# E2E inventory

Closed on 2026-11-19: `dms-control` and the verify harness are deleted in [#1172](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms/pull/1172). The tables below record where each line went.

Decision 13 of the trunk run deletes `dms-control` (`packages/dms/bin/dms-control.mjs` and `packages/dms/bin/control/`) and the DMS verify harness only once everything they run has a new home. This file lists every line they run, repo by repo, with its destination and its status. A port pull request updates the status of the lines it moves, in the same pull request.

Snapshot: the default branch of every repository on 2026-11-19, and the latest nightly CI run before that date.

## How to read a table

- **Line**: a journey id, a formal check, or a harness step. A journey is the unit `dms-control journey` and the kit run.
- **Kind**: `browser` when the journey opens a dashboard tab, `HTTP` when it only calls the API or reads the mail sink, `command line` when it only runs commands or reads files.
- **What it checks**: the journey's `title`, then its known failures and its run flags (`dev dashboard`, `backend dev|watch|production`, `no standby`, `last`, `shared database`).
- **Runner today**:
  - `kit`: `pnpm e2e`, Playwright through `@antelopejs/dms/e2e`, which starts one `dms-control up` run and drives each journey with `dms-control`'s journey runner. `dms-control journey <run> <feature>` runs the same journey by hand.
  - `dms-control journey`: only `dms-control journey`; no CI job runs it.
  - `own Playwright`: the repo's own Playwright stack, with no `dms-control` at run time.
  - `antelopejs-verify`: the `antelopejs-verify` bin of `@antelopejs/tooling-configs`.
  - `dms-control formal`, `dms-control up --prepare`: the subcommand named.
- **Destination**:
  - `Playwright`: the browser journey becomes the Playwright test `<feature> › <journey id>` in the repo's e2e suite (`e2e/`), tagged `@smoke` when the @smoke column says so. The kit's `registerJourneys()` names its tests this way today.
  - `integration <file>`: the HTTP or command-line journey becomes the integration test `<journey id>` in that file of the repo's test suite: the repo's integration test folder plus `<feature>.journeys.test.ts`. A port may group the tests in other files of that folder and records the file here. `was @smoke` marks a journey the e2e smoke set loses: the port tags another browser journey in its place.
  - a formal check becomes an integration test run by CI: its existing CI job, or a new one.
  - `deleted: duplicate of <x>`: a line that `<x>` already runs.
- **Status**:
  - `todo`: not at its destination yet, or at it with no green CI run to prove it; the reason follows the colon.
  - `ported in <PR>`: the port pull request is open.
  - `green in CI`: it runs at its destination and passed in the latest nightly run named in the repo's header, or in the merge queue run of the pull request that moved it, which that pull request links.
  - `accepted without CI by Antony (private repo, billing)`: the line runs at its destination, and Antony accepted it with no CI run to prove it.
  - `deleted: duplicate of <x>`: its destination is `deleted`, and nothing is left to port. A browser journey that the kit runs is at its destination already: what still ties it to `dms-control` is the kit, listed in [The kit](#the-kit-antelopejsdmse2e).

The kind comes from running each journey against a recording context with every package import, process spawn and network module stubbed, plus the journey's own source and entry point. The rules are mechanical: a port that finds a line in the wrong kind moves it and fixes its row.

dms-lang counts the four Playwright journeys the 2026-11-19 snapshot missed (rows 183 to 186 of its section). The ports moved eight lines from HTTP to browser because they open a tab: dms-api rows 30 to 34, dms-automation rows 174 and 197, and dms-frontend row 70.

Re-check a count:

```bash
# features of a verify skill (one Markdown file per feature)
ls .agents/skills/verify-*/features/*.md | grep -vc README.md
# journeys of a kit repo: build config, then the dev config where the repo has one
pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm exec playwright test -c e2e --list | tail -1
pnpm exec playwright test -c e2e/playwright.dev.config.ts --list | tail -1
# dms-database (own Playwright)
pnpm exec playwright test -c e2e --list | tail -1
# dms-antelope-cloud (own Playwright)
grep -cE '^test\(' e2e/*.spec.ts
# verify-dms-builder of dms-assistant, which no Playwright config loads
cat .agents/skills/verify-dms-builder/journeys/*.mjs | grep -cE '^    id: "'
# formal checks of a skill
grep -cE '^\s+name: "' .agents/skills/verify-*/verify.config.ts
# template journeys: the journeys: list of each feature file
for f in .agents/skills/verify-template/features/*.md; do awk '/^---$/{n++; next} n==1' "$f" | awk '/^journeys:/{on=1; next} /^[a-z]+:/{on=0} on && /^ *- /'; done | wc -l
```

The counts of this file match `playwright test --list` on dms (112) and dms-saas (81), id for id, and the test counts of the latest nightly reports of dms, dms-frontend, dms-api, dms-lang, dms-assistant and dms-database.

### Run-time ties to the verify folders

The lines above are green, but these suites still read a verify skill folder or run `dms-control`. The deletion breaks them unless each one moves first:

| Repo | What still reads a verify folder or `dms-control` |
|---|---|
| dms-saas, dms-marketing, dms-mailing, dms-automation, dms-media, dms-frontend, dms-api, dms-lang, dms-assistant | no `e2e/journeys.config.ts`, so the kit falls back to `.agents/skills/verify-*/verify.config.ts` for the playground, the `prepare` commands, the smoke feature and the feature list (#1166). The playgrounds live under `.agents/skills/verify-*/playground`. |
| dms-api | `tests/helpers/backend.ts` starts `.agents/skills/verify-dms-api/playground`, and `tests/package.test.ts` runs `dms-control map` |
| dms-assistant | `e2e/playwright.config.ts`, `e2e/playwright.dev.config.ts` and `e2e/builder/playwright.config.ts` name their verify skill, and `tests/integration/assistant.journeys.test.mjs` and `builder.journeys.test.mjs` load `.agents/skills/verify-dms-assistant` and `.agents/skills/verify-dms-builder` |
| dms-automation | `tests/feature-map.test.cjs` runs `dms-control map`, and `e2e:build` links `node_modules` into `.agents/skills/verify-dms-automation/playground` |
| dms-database | `src/test/helpers/featureMap.ts` reads `.agents/skills/verify-dms-database/verify.config.ts` |
| dms-lang | `tests/feature-map.test.mjs` runs `dms-control`, and `tests/key-writes.test.mjs` (row 181) runs `.agents/skills/verify-dms-lang-key-writes/check.mjs` |
| dms-mailing | `test/verify-harness.test.mjs` runs `dms-control`, `e2e:build` installs into `.agents/skills/verify-dms-mailing`, and `scripts/check-verify-deps-lockfile.mts` reads its `deps/` |
| dms-media | `tests/feature-map.test.mjs` runs `dms-control map` |
| dms-saas | `scripts/checks/feature-map.mts` runs `dms-control map` |

Accepted without CI by Antony: 15 lines, all of dms-antelope-cloud. Its tests have not moved, but the repository is private and its Actions runs stay blocked by a GitHub billing problem, so no CI run on `main` proves them green:

- dms-antelope-cloud 1: `antelope-cloud.spec.ts` › the owner browses the module and sees the cloud API status
- dms-antelope-cloud 2: `antelope-cloud.spec.ts` › the owner connects the workspace to AntelopeJS Cloud, chooses its project, then disconnects
- dms-antelope-cloud 3: `deployments.spec.ts` › the owner follows a deployment live, rolls production back and reads its logs
- dms-antelope-cloud 4: `environments-setup.spec.ts` › the owner puts a new project online through the first-run setup
- dms-antelope-cloud 5: `environments-setup.spec.ts` › the owner promotes Staging to Production by a push of env/production, and a plan with no room refuses a new environment
- dms-antelope-cloud 6: `environments-setup.spec.ts` › the Overview says what runs where, a failed build, and a project with one environment
- dms-antelope-cloud 7: `publish-cloud.spec.ts` › the owner sees the Redis the version adds, publishes it, and follows AntelopeJS Cloud to live without a reload
- dms-antelope-cloud 8: `publish-cloud.spec.ts` › a deployment that fails on AntelopeJS Cloud says which step failed and what visitors still see
- dms-antelope-cloud 9: `publish-cloud.spec.ts` › Deployments opens with what each environment serves
- dms-antelope-cloud 10: `publish-environments.spec.ts` › the owner publishes to Staging right after the setup
- dms-antelope-cloud 11: `publish-environments.spec.ts` › the owner publishes straight to Production when it is the project's only environment
- dms-antelope-cloud 12: `publishing.spec.ts` › the owner publishes the unpublished changes as a version
- dms-antelope-cloud 13: `resources.spec.ts` › the owner reviews the plan of an environment, applies it, and sees the Free plan refuse it
- dms-antelope-cloud 14: `rollback-with-database.spec.ts` › the owner rolls production back when it runs a database, and reads why a built image was not deployed
- dms-antelope-cloud 15: `variables.spec.ts` › the owner reads each environment's variables and replaces a secret it never sees again

## dms-control

`node packages/dms/bin/dms-control.mjs` with no argument prints these subcommands. The verify skills of the DMS repos call them through `$C <subcommand>`; the DMS repos install `dms-control` as the bin of `@antelopejs/dms`.

| # | Subcommand | What it does | Kind | Used by | Destination | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `up` | lays the checkout out again, starts a `mongod` replica set, a mail sink, the backend behind a forwarder and the dashboard on free ports, with the dashboard build cache | command line | every skill; the kit's global setup (`e2e-run.mjs`) | the kit's `globalSetup`, which starts the run through `startRun()` (see [The kit](#the-kit-antelopejsdmse2e)) | green in CI: [#1167 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms/actions/runs/44512085569) |
| 2 | `up --prepare` | runs the skill's `prepare` commands (install, builds) before `up` | command line | every skill; the kit unless `DMS_E2E_PREBUILT=1` | the kit's global setup runs the `prepare` commands of `e2e/journeys.config.ts` before `playwright test` (`DMS_E2E_PREBUILT=1` skips them) | green in CI: [#1167 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms/actions/runs/44512085569) |
| 3 | `doctor` | checks the recorded processes hold their ports, both servers answer, the API forwards to the run's backend, the builds are newer than their sources, the frontend build matches its key, HEAD has not moved, the admin session is valid | command line | every DMS repo's skill | deleted: duplicate of the `webServer` readiness checks (`url` answering) and of the build step that runs before the suite | deleted: duplicate of the `webServer` readiness checks (`url` answering) and of the build step that runs before the suite |
| 4 | `onboard` | creates the run's administrator through `POST /api/onboarding/register`, then signs it in | HTTP | every DMS repo's skill; every journey without `onboard: false` | a kit fixture that onboards the administrator of each fresh database | green in CI: [#1167 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms/actions/runs/44512085569) |
| 5 | `browser` | one step on the run's long-lived browser: `goto`, `click`, `fill`, `snapshot` (ARIA tree), `screenshot`, `login`… | browser | every DMS repo's skill (by hand) | deleted: duplicate of `playwright test --ui`, `--debug` and `playwright codegen` against the suite's servers | deleted: duplicate of `playwright test --ui`, `--debug` and `playwright codegen` against the suite's servers |
| 6 | `api` | an authenticated API call as the administrator, another account or anonymous | HTTP | every DMS repo's skill (by hand) | `dms.api()` of the kit's `dms` fixture | green in CI: [#1167 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms/actions/runs/44512085569) |
| 7 | `mail` | prints the messages the run's mail sink captured | HTTP | dms, dms-frontend, dms-lang, dms-mailing skills (by hand) | `dms.mail()` of the kit's `dms` fixture, on the sink the global setup starts | green in CI: [#1167 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms/actions/runs/44512085569) |
| 8 | `reset` | moves the run onto an empty database and a backend that served nothing | command line | every DMS repo's skill (by hand) | a kit fixture: a fresh database and backend per test, as `runJourney` gives each journey today | green in CI: [#1167 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms/actions/runs/44512085569) |
| 9 | `down` | stops every recorded process, drops the run's databases, keeps the evidence | command line | every DMS repo's skill; the kit's global teardown | the teardown the kit's global setup returns, which stops every process of the run | green in CI: [#1167 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms/actions/runs/44512085569) |
| 10 | `timings` | prints how long each phase of the run took, from `evidence/timings.jsonl` | command line | dms skill | deleted: duplicate of Playwright's JSON reporter durations and `--reporter=list` timings | deleted: duplicate of Playwright's JSON reporter durations and `--reporter=list` timings |
| 11 | `map` | checks that the feature map's `covers:` globs cover every user-facing file | command line | dms CI (`ci.yml` step `node packages/dms/bin/dms-control.mjs map`), dms-api skill | deleted with the feature maps it checks; the e2e suite's test list replaces them | deleted: duplicate of the e2e suite's test list (`playwright test -c e2e --list`) |
| 12 | `plan` | maps the diff to the features to run, the shared files that force every feature, and the unmapped surfaces | command line | every DMS repo's skill | deleted: duplicate of the CI tier selection of `ci-node.yml` (`fast`: affected tests, `full`/`queue`: whole suite) | deleted: duplicate of the CI tier selection of `ci-node.yml` (`fast`: affected tests, `full`/`queue`: whole suite) |
| 13 | `journey` | runs a feature's journeys on a run and records evidence and `result.json` | browser, HTTP, command line | every DMS repo's skill; the kit calls its runner (`runJourney`) for each test | deleted: duplicate of `playwright test -g <id>`; the journey runner moves into the kit | deleted: duplicate of `playwright test -g <id>`; the journey runner moves into the kit |
| 14 | `doors` | prints the `One-way-door:` line for a patch from the path rules of `verify.config.ts` | command line | every DMS repo's skill | deleted: duplicate of `antelopejs-verify doors` (`@antelopejs/tooling-configs`), which reads the same `doors` rules | deleted: duplicate of `antelopejs-verify doors` (`@antelopejs/tooling-configs`), which reads the same `doors` rules |
| 15 | `formal` | runs the formal checks whose covered files the diff touches (or all with `--all`) | command line | skills of dms, dms-saas, dms-marketing, dms-mailing, dms-automation, dms-media, dms-frontend, dms-api, dms-lang, dms-database | deleted: duplicate of the CI formal jobs: `checks/affected-packages.mjs` reads the checks from `checks/formal-checks.ts` and runs each in `formal`, `formal-generated` or the builder shards; the `formal` job runs the `--full` configurations on the nightly and manual runs | deleted: duplicate of the CI formal jobs: `checks/affected-packages.mjs` reads the checks from `checks/formal-checks.ts` and runs each in `formal`, `formal-generated` or the builder shards; the `formal` job runs the `--full` configurations on the nightly and manual runs |
| 16 | `verdict` | not a `dms-control` subcommand: the skills of dms-assistant, dms-automation and dms-marketing name it, and `dms-control` prints its usage | none | dms-assistant, dms-automation, dms-marketing skills (stale docs) | deleted with the skills | deleted: duplicate of the usage `dms-control` prints (no such subcommand) |

Its own tests (`packages/dms/bin/control/**/*.test.mjs`, 36 files) test the harness, not a product: they go with it, except `e2e-suite.test.mjs`, `journey.test.mjs`, `journey-judge.test.mjs` and `tab.test.mjs`, which move with the code they test into the kit.

## The kit: @antelopejs/dms/e2e

`@antelopejs/dms/e2e` (`packages/dms/bin/control/e2e.mjs`, [E2E.md](../packages/dms/E2E.md)) exports `e2eConfig()` and `registerJourneys()`. Its config's `globalSetup` (`e2e-run.mjs`) runs `dms-control up --prepare`, and each test drives its journey through `runJourney` of `journey.mjs`. Its import closure is 41 of the 46 non-test modules of `bin/control` (all but `commands-verify.mjs`, `prepared-workspace.mjs`, `verify/doors.mjs`, `verify/formal.mjs` and `verify/map.mjs`), plus `@playwright/test` and `playwright-core`.

Kit users: dms, dms-saas, dms-marketing, dms-mailing, dms-automation, dms-media, dms-frontend, dms-api, dms-lang and dms-assistant (`e2e/playwright.config.ts`, and `e2e/playwright.dev.config.ts` in dms-assistant). dms-database and dms-antelope-cloud run their own Playwright stacks.

| # | What the kit needs | Modules in `bin/control` today (lines) | Replacement in a standard Playwright setup | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | the suite's config and its journey tests | `e2e.mjs` (179), `e2e-suite.mjs` (129), `journey-load.mjs` (104), `options.mjs` (37) | stays in the kit: `e2eConfig()` returns a `defineConfig` with `globalSetup`/`webServer`; `registerJourneys()` keeps its API and the journey fields `tag`, `knownFailures`, `last`, `frontend` | green in CI: [#1167 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms/actions/runs/44512085569) |

| 14 | the run self-check | `doctor.mjs` (347) | deleted: duplicate of `webServer` readiness and the build step | deleted: duplicate of `webServer` readiness and the build step |

#1162 moves every module of rows 1 to 13 into the kit (`packages/dms/e2e/`) as it is, rather than replacing it as the column proposes: the stack keeps its copy of the playground, its standby backends and its content-keyed build cache. The kit spawns its services through `packages/dms/e2e/service.mjs`, its global setup starts the run through `startRun()` of `@antelopejs/dms/e2e/run`, and an oxlint rule refuses any import of `packages/dms/bin/` from `packages/dms/e2e/` or `e2e/`. `doctor.mjs` (row 14) stays in `bin/control/`, outside the kit's closure.

Done for the kit means: no file the `./e2e` export reaches imports `bin/control/` outside the kit, and every kit user's suite passes on the new version by a version bump.

## dms-lang

Skill folders: `verify-dms-lang`, `verify-dms-lang-key-writes`. Latest nightly e2e: [2026-11-19 nightly](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms-lang/actions/runs/44444280873): green.

Rows 183 to 186 are Playwright journeys the 2026-11-19 snapshot missed, found by #157.

`verify-dms-lang-key-writes` has no feature map or journeys: it is the bounded check `check.mjs` that `verify-dms-lang` lists as the formal check `key-writes`, in the rows below.

## dms-assistant

Skill folders: `verify-dms-assistant`, `verify-dms-builder`. Latest nightly e2e: [2026-11-19 nightly](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms-assistant/actions/runs/44444088090): red: 52 passed, 3 failed.

`verify-dms-builder` runs only through `dms-control journey`, with its own playground under the skill folder: `e2e/playwright.config.ts` names `verify-dms-assistant`, so no CI job runs these journeys. Its rows are prefixed `verify-dms-builder:`.

## dms-database

Skill folders: `verify-dms-database`. Latest nightly e2e: [2026-11-19 nightly](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms-database/actions/runs/44444276893): red: 146 passed, 5 failed.

Its journeys already run on its own Playwright stack (`e2e/stack.ts`, `e2e/fixtures.ts`, `e2e/specs/<feature>.spec.ts`), not on the kit. `dms-control` serves only the skill's interactive commands (`up`, `browser`, `api`, `plan`, `doors`); `map` and `journey` no longer work for it, as its SKILL.md says. Its stack is the model the kit can follow.

## dms-antelope-cloud

Skill folders: `verify-dms-antelope-cloud`. Latest nightly e2e: [2026-11-18 nightly](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/dms-antelope-cloud/actions/runs/44222904155): green (the 2026-11-19 run never started its `tier` job). Since then a GitHub billing problem blocks the Actions runs of this private repository, so Antony accepted its lines without CI.

`verify-dms-antelope-cloud` has 11 feature files and no journeys modules: its journeys are the Playwright tests of `e2e/*.spec.ts`, on the repo's own stack (`e2e/stack.ts`, a mock cloud API), with no `dms-control`. The feature column is the feature file each test belongs to.

## template-dms-demo

Skill folder: `verify-template`.

`verify-template` is the shared template skill of `@antelopejs/tooling-configs`: `antelopejs-verify` runs its journeys (`journeys` in `verify.config.ts`, plus the built-ins `scaffold-dev` and `scaffold-checks`), not `dms-control`. CI runs `antelopejs-verify journey <run> project locked-build` in the `Locked build` job (green in the [2026-11-19 nightly](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/template-dms-demo/actions/runs/44444215243)), and `next-canary.yml` runs the `smoke` feature against the `next` previews every night ([2026-11-18](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/template-dms-demo/actions/runs/44232299207): green). The canary watches previews of the `next` branch, which the trunk run retires: the canary's new trigger is a preview published from `main`. A template journey scaffolds a project with `ajs project init` or `ajs module init` and drives it, so its destination is an integration test of the template repo.

| # | Feature | Line | Kind | What it checks | Runner today | Destination | @smoke | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | backend | `home-page` | HTTP | the home page lays out the demo's hero and one block per section of `content/home/`, serves each section's Markdown in the reader's language with English as the fallback, and counts its figures from what is registered: the menu pages, controllers and modules match counts the journey derives from the scaffold and from the API | antelopejs-verify | integration test `.github/scripts/template/backend.test.mts › home-page` |  | green in CI: [#199 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/template-dms-demo/actions/runs/44499679661) |
| 2 | backend | `helio-data` | command line | Helio's tables live in the `template-dms-demo__helio_*` collections the template names, and its customers hold the fields `Customer` declares: the list answers every stored row, the guarded routes refuse a request without a token, a business needs a valid VAT number, and only an archived customer can be deleted | antelopejs-verify | integration test `.github/scripts/template/backend.test.mts › helio-data` |  | green in CI: [#199 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/template-dms-demo/actions/runs/44499679661) |

## template-dms-adonis

Skill folder: `verify-template`.

`verify-template` is the shared template skill of `@antelopejs/tooling-configs`: `antelopejs-verify` runs its journeys (`journeys` in `verify.config.ts`, plus the built-ins `scaffold-dev` and `scaffold-checks`), not `dms-control`. No CI job runs a journey. A template journey scaffolds a project with `ajs project init` or `ajs module init` and drives it, so its destination is an integration test of the template repo.

| # | Feature | Line | Kind | What it checks | Runner today | Destination | @smoke | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| 3 | demo | `demo-content` | HTTP | the demo module boots inside AdonisJS: its home page is a DMS page, its frontend module reaches the dashboard and Helio's fixtures land in MongoDB | antelopejs-verify | integration test `.github/scripts/template/demo.test.ts › demo-content` |  | green in CI: [#71 queue run](https://github.com/AntelopeJS/template-dms-adonis/actions/runs/44497466573) |

## Repos on antelopejs-verify outside the DMS list

These repos run the shared `verify-module`, `verify-interface` or `verify-template` skill through `antelopejs-verify` (`tooling-configs/src/bin/verify.ts`), with no `dms-control` and no browser journey. Decision 13 does not delete `antelopejs-verify`, so their lines stay where they are; this table counts them so a later decision can size the work. `antelopejs` runs its own harness (`verify-antelopejs`, `bin/journeys`).
